I don't really notice doing that with my friends or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking, like spending money or doing something that might be risky?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I don't really spend money like that. And... I don't know, I don't really do risky things either. So... no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your trouble sitting still and impulsivity are causing problems for you in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah... um, I guess it is. At clinicals, mostly. My evaluations have kind of... taken a hit. They actually put me on a formal remediation plan recently. The supervisors say my behavior disrupts patient flow and... makes teamwork harder. So... yeah. I'm just worried it could cost me my place in the program if I don't get it under control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty waiting for your turn in line or when waiting for something?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. Like... in lab practice. If I have to wait for a machine to open up, I just... I get really edgy. I end up stepping forward a lot, or... checking the time. I'll ask if I can go next, even if it's not my turn yet. They've... called me out on it a few times. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless or have trouble sitting still at home or when you're not in a clinical or academic setting?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't really... noticed. When I'm at home, I just kind of do whatever, so... I don't know. I guess I don't really pay attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks at work or in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I usually just do what I'm supposed to do. I mean, I get things done eventually. I haven't really had anyone say anything about that, so... yeah, I guess it's fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control your restlessness and impulsivity when you're in a situation where you really need to, like in a important meeting or a critical patient care situation?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I guess I try to? I haven't really... been in a lot of super critical situations yet, since I'm mostly just doing clinicals. So... I'm not really sure how well I control it. I just kind of... try to get through it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your mind on what you're doing?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... if I'm doing something, I just do it. I haven't really noticed my mind wandering off or anything like that. So... yeah, I think it's fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting at home or doing quiet activities?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. Even if it's something I like doing... like editing photos or sketching. I don't know, I just... I can't really stay quiet for very long. I start tapping or humming, or I'll just start talking to whoever is around. I end up getting up a lot. People at home or in the lab... they usually tell me to just settle down. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sitting still or feel restless when you're with friends or in other social situations outside of work or school?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think so. I don't really go to a lot of quiet places besides the ones we already talked about. So... I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your trouble sitting still and impulsivity are more problematic for you than difficulties with attention or following instructions?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess... I don't know which one is worse.
